At first his colleagues saw his surge in energy and big, unconventional sales ideas as a plus, and he leaned into late nights of data crunching and networking at craft‑beer events, letting sleep and basic routines slide. Over the month that followed, his behavior at work became increasingly erratic—grandiose pitches that ignored client needs, impulsive spending on \"can’t‑fail\" marketing ideas, and tense clashes with his manager—while friends noticed his conversations drifting into bizarre, fixed beliefs about having a special role in transforming the Providence waterfront economy. He finally came to psychiatric attention when his company’s HR department, alarmed by several alarming emails to major clients and a disorganized presentation that led to a near‑loss of a key contract, insisted he see an emergency evaluator before returning to work.\n- Chief Complaint: “I’ve been way more driven and wired than usual for weeks, like I can’t slow down or stop starting new projects, and now it’s starting to blow up my job.”\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S020 (Hallucinations): Lately when I’m up late working on these big waterfront plans, I keep hearing this deep, calm voice that isn’t mine telling me I’ve been chosen to “wake up” Providence’s economy and that the clients are all secretly testing if I’m ready, even when I’m alone in my apartment with the TV off.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): It’s weird, because even though I’m running around all day, I’ve completely lost interest in stuff I used to love—my soccer league, hiking on Sundays, even grabbing beers at shows—because all of it feels pointless compared to this mission I’m supposed to be on with work and the waterfront deal.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): I’ve been so keyed up and focused on these ideas that I’ll realize it’s 5 p.m. and I haven’t eaten anything all day, and then I just grab a granola bar or skip dinner because I don’t feel hungry at all anymore, even though my jeans are hanging off me now.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I just lie there buzzing, maybe getting two or three hours of broken sleep because my brain won’t shut up, and even if I do doze off I’m awake again at 3 or 4 a.m. pacing around and checking my email like it’s the middle of the workday.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): I can’t sit still—I pace my apartment, tap my feet in meetings, keep jumping up to reorganize my bag or rewrite my notes, and even on planes for work I’m constantly fidgeting and getting up, like my body can’t handle being stopped for more than a minute.\n- S029 (Fatigue): At the same time, underneath all this running around, I feel this deep worn-out heaviness, like my muscles are full of sand and my brain is fried, but I still push myself because I feel like I’ll fall apart if I slow down.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): When things at work started going sideways, I got stuck on this idea that I ruined the whole region’s business prospects and that if the account fails it’s basically my fault that everyone on the waterfront—from restaurant owners to dock workers—won’t be able to feed their families, like I’m personally responsible for all of that.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): On the worst days, when I’m convinced I’ve blown my shot and wrecked my career, I catch myself thinking it would be easier if I just didn’t wake up or if I stepped in front of a bus after leaving the office, not because I have a set plan, but because the pressure and shame feel that overwhelming.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): For the past few weeks I’ve either felt insanely pumped up, like I’m on top of the world and everybody else is moving in slow motion, or I flip on a dime and snap at people over tiny things—like my manager asking a basic question—and I can’t seem to find any middle ground.\n- S034 (Increased_Activity): I’ve been running on this nonstop drive where I’m juggling new sales strategies, side projects, and big “revamp the waterfront” schemes all at once, staying up all night cranking out slide decks and emails, and even when HR told me to back off I went right back to plotting three new campaigns because I literally can’t make myself stop.\n- S037 (Pressured_Speech): Once I start talking, especially about my big ideas for the region or how we can double sales, I just go and go, jumping from one thing to another so fast that people at work can’t get a word in and I’ll keep talking over them because it feels physically uncomfortable to shut up.\n- S038 (Racing_Thoughts): My thoughts are racing so hard that in the middle of a client call I’ll be connecting ten different marketing angles, thinking about redesigning their whole supply chain, planning a waterfront festival, and drafting follow-up emails in my head all at once, and I can’t slow any of it down.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): I’ll be in the middle of an important presentation and suddenly I’m distracted by a flicker on someone’s phone screen, a car horn outside, or a pattern in the carpet, and then I’m off on a tangent about some new idea, totally losing track of what I was supposed to be saying.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- delusions_and_hallucinations_occur_exclusively_during_the_manic_episode: Those voices about being “chosen” and my fixed belief that I’m meant to save the whole Providence waterfront only started when I got super amped up and barely sleeping after the promotion, and in the calmer stretches before this I never heard voices or thought I had some special mission.\n- functional_impairment: HR pulled me off client-facing work and made me see an emergency evaluator after my rambling emails and chaotic presentation nearly cost us a major account, and outside of work I’ve basically dropped my friends and hobbies because everything except these grand plans feels irrelevant.\n- duration: All of this—the extreme highs with the crashy, guilty lows—has really been going on since about a month after I took on the new regional account and started pushing myself nonstop.\n- depressive_episode_duration: The heavy, guilty, checked-out feelings and thoughts about not wanting to wake up have been hanging around for roughly the last four months, getting worse as things at work started to fall apart.\n\nRules:\n- If the patient can specifically answer based on the grabbing beers at shows—because all of it feels pointless compared to this mission I’m supposed to be on with work and the waterfront deal.\n- S026 (Weight_Appetite_Change): I’ve been so keyed up and focused on these ideas that I’ll realize it’s 5 p.m. and I haven’t eaten anything all day, and then I just grab a granola bar or skip dinner because I don’t feel hungry at all anymore, even though my jeans are hanging off me now.\n- S027 (Sleep_Disturbance): Most nights I just lie there buzzing, maybe getting two or three hours of broken sleep because my brain won’t shut up, and even if I do doze off I’m awake again at 3 or 4 a.m. pacing around and checking my email like it’s the middle of the workday.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): I can’t sit still—I pace my apartment, tap my feet in up or if I stepped in front of a bus after leaving the office, not because I have a set plan, but because the pressure and shame feel that overwhelming.\n- S033 (Elevated_Irritable_Mood): For the past few weeks I’ve either felt insanely pumped up, like I’m on top of the world and everybody else is moving in slow motion, or I flip on a dime and snap at people over tiny things—like my manager asking
